---
title: "kubernetes_batch_job"
description: "`kubernetes_batch_job` runs one or more pods to completion for a real, one-off or parallelizable task..."
---

`kubernetes_batch_job` runs one or more pods to completion for a real, one-off or parallelizable task, tracking real success and retrying on failure -- the fit for a batch computation or a migration script, not a long-running service.

## Example

<Tabs>
  <Tab title="Go">
    ```go
    package main

    import (
    	ubx "github.com/ubiquex/ubx-sdk-go/runtime"
    	batch "github.com/ubiquex/ubx-sdk-kubernetes/sdk/go/kubernetes/batch"
    )

    func main() {
    	ubx.Main(ubx.Stack("example", func() {
    		ubx.Intent(ubx.IntentInfo{Summary: "example own job"})

    		ubx.Resource(batch.Job, "example", batch.JobConfig{
    			Name:      "example",
    			Namespace: "example",
    		})
    	}))
    }
    ```
  </Tab>
  <Tab title="TypeScript">
    ```typescript
    import { intent, resource, stack } from "@ubx/sdk";
    import { Job } from "@ubx/sdk-kubernetes/kubernetes/batch/job";

    export default stack("example", () => {
      intent({ summary: "example own job" });

      resource(Job, "example", {
        name: "example",
        namespace: "example",
      });
    });
    ```
  </Tab>
  <Tab title="Python">
    ```python
    import ubx_sdk as ubx
    from ubx.kubernetes.batch import Job, JobConfig

    def describe():
        ubx.intent("example own job")

        ubx.resource(Job, "example", JobConfig(
            name="example",
            namespace="example",
        ))

    if __name__ == "__main__":
        ubx.run("example", describe)
    ```
  </Tab>
  <Tab title="Markdown">
    ```
    Create kubernetes_batch_job called "example", with namespace
    example.
    ```
  </Tab>
</Tabs>

      Must be empty before the object is deleted from the registry. Each entry is an identifier for the responsible component that will remove the entry from the list. If the deletionTimestamp of the object is non-nil, entries in this list can only be removed. Finalizers may be processed and removed in any order. Order is NOT enforced because it introduces significant risk of stuck finalizers. finalizers is a shared field, any actor with permission can reorder it. If the finalizer list is processed in order, then this can lead to a situation in which the component responsible for the first finalizer in the list is waiting for a signal (field value, external system, or other) produced by a component responsible for a finalizer later in the list, resulting in a deadlock. Without enforced ordering finalizers are free to order amongst themselves and are not vulnerable to ordering changes in the list. Optional.

<ResponseField name="spec" type="object">
  JobSpec describes how the job execution will look like. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes.
  <Expandable title="properties">
    <ResponseField name="active_deadline_seconds" type="number">
      Specifies the duration in seconds relative to the startTime that the job may be continuously active before the system tries to terminate it; value must be positive integer. If a Job is suspended (at creation or through an update), this timer will effectively be stopped and reset when the Job is resumed again. Optional.
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="backoff_limit" type="number">
      Specifies the number of retries before marking this job failed. Defaults to 6, unless backoffLimitPerIndex (only Indexed Job) is specified. When backoffLimitPerIndex is specified, backoffLimit defaults to 2147483647. Optional.
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="backoff_limit_per_index" type="number">
      Specifies the limit for the number of retries within an index before marking this index as failed. When enabled the number of failures per index is kept in the pod's batch.kubernetes.io/job-index-failure-count annotation. It can only be set when Job's completionMode=Indexed, and the Pod's restart policy is Never. The field is immutable. Optional.
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="completion_mode" type="string">
      completionMode specifies how Pod completions are tracked. It can be `NonIndexed` (default) or `Indexed`. `NonIndexed` means that the Job is considered complete when there have been .spec.completions successfully completed Pods. Each Pod completion is homologous to each other. `Indexed` means that the Pods of a Job get an associated completion index from 0 to (.spec.completions - 1), available in the annotation batch.kubernetes.io/job-completion-index. The Job is considered complete when there is one successfully completed Pod for each index. When value is `Indexed`, .spec.completions must be specified and `.spec.parallelism` must be less than or equal to 10^5. In addition, The Pod name takes the form `$(job-name)-$(index)-$(random-string)`, the Pod hostname takes the form `$(job-name)-$(index)`. More completion modes can be added in the future. If the Job controller observes a mode that it doesn't recognize, which is possible during upgrades due to version skew, the controller skips updates for the Job. Optional.
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="completions" type="number">
      Specifies the desired number of successfully finished pods the job should be run with. Setting to null means that the success of any pod signals the success of all pods, and allows parallelism to have any positive value. Setting to 1 means that parallelism is limited to 1 and the success of that pod signals the success of the job. More info: https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/workloads/controllers/jobs-run-to-completion/ Optional.
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="managed_by" type="string">
      ManagedBy field indicates the controller that manages a Job. The k8s Job controller reconciles jobs which don't have this field at all or the field value is the reserved string `kubernetes.io/job-controller`, but skips reconciling Jobs with a custom value for this field. The value must be a valid domain-prefixed path (e.g. acme.io/foo) - all characters before the first "/" must be a valid subdomain as defined by RFC 1123. All characters trailing the first "/" must be valid HTTP Path characters as defined by RFC 3986. The value cannot exceed 63 characters. This field is immutable. Optional.
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="manual_selector" type="bool">
      manualSelector controls generation of pod labels and pod selectors. Leave `manualSelector` unset unless you are certain what you are doing. When false or unset, the system pick labels unique to this job and appends those labels to the pod template. When true, the user is responsible for picking unique labels and specifying the selector. Failure to pick a unique label may cause this and other jobs to not function correctly. However, You may see `manualSelector=true` in jobs that were created with the old `extensions/v1beta1` API. More info: https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/workloads/controllers/jobs-run-to-completion/#specifying-your-own-pod-selector Optional.
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="max_failed_indexes" type="number">
      Specifies the maximal number of failed indexes before marking the Job as failed, when backoffLimitPerIndex is set. Once the number of failed indexes exceeds this number the entire Job is marked as Failed and its execution is terminated. When left as null the job continues execution of all of its indexes and is marked with the `Complete` Job condition. It can only be specified when backoffLimitPerIndex is set. It can be null or up to completions. It is required and must be less than or equal to 10^4 when is completions greater than 10^5. Optional.
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="parallelism" type="number">
      Specifies the maximum desired number of pods the job should run at any given time. The actual number of pods running in steady state will be less than this number when ((.spec.completions - .status.successful) &lt; .spec.parallelism), i.e. when the work left to do is less than max parallelism. More info: https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/workloads/controllers/jobs-run-to-completion/ Optional.
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="pod_failure_policy" type="object">
      PodFailurePolicy describes how failed pods influence the backoffLimit. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="rules" type="list(object)" required>
          A list of pod failure policy rules. The rules are evaluated in order. Once a rule matches a Pod failure, the remaining of the rules are ignored. When no rule matches the Pod failure, the default handling applies - the counter of pod failures is incremented and it is checked against the backoffLimit. At most 20 elements are allowed. Required.
          <Expandable title="properties">
            <ResponseField name="action" type="string">
              Specifies the action to take when a pod failure matches the rule. Allowed values are `FailJob`, `Ignore`, and `Count`. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="on_exit_codes" type="object">
              The on_exit_codes block specifies the container exit code condition that triggers the pod failure policy. It includes the container name, an operator (In or NotIn), and the list of exit code values that match the rule. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
              <Expandable title="properties">
                <ResponseField name="container_name" type="string">
                  The name of the container to which the exit code rule applies. If not specified, the rule applies to all containers. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="operator" type="string">
                  Operator defines the relationship between the container exit code and the values in the list. Valid values are 'In' (match if the exit code is in the list) and 'NotIn' (match if the exit code is not in the list). Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="values" type="list(number)">
                  List of container exit codes that the rule matches. If any container in the pod exits with one of these codes, the PodFailurePolicy rule applies. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
                </ResponseField>
              </Expandable>
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="on_pod_conditions" type="list(object)">
              A list of pod conditions that, when matched, cause the pod failure policy rule to apply. Each item specifies a condition type (e.g., DisruptionTarget) and a status (True, False, or Unknown) that the condition must have for the rule to take effect. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
              <Expandable title="properties">
                <ResponseField name="status" type="string">
                  The status of the pod condition that must be matched for the policy rule to apply. Possible values are True, False, or Unknown. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="type" type="string">
                  The type of the Pod condition to match, such as DisruptionTarget, which indicates the pod was evicted due to a disruption. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
                </ResponseField>
              </Expandable>
            </ResponseField>
          </Expandable>
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="pod_replacement_policy" type="string">
      podReplacementPolicy specifies when to create replacement Pods. Possible values are: - TerminatingOrFailed means that we recreate pods when they are terminating (has a metadata.deletionTimestamp) or failed. - Failed means to wait until a previously created Pod is fully terminated (has phase Failed or Succeeded) before creating a replacement Pod. When using podFailurePolicy, Failed is the the only allowed value. TerminatingOrFailed and Failed are allowed values when podFailurePolicy is not in use. Optional.
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="scheduling" type="object">
      JobSchedulingConfiguration composes the reusable workload-aware scheduling building blocks. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="disruption_mode" type="object">
          WorkloadPodGroupDisruptionMode defines how individual pods within a group can be disrupted. Exactly one mode must be set. Optional.
          <Expandable title="properties">
            <ResponseField name="all" type="any">
              WorkloadPodGroupAllDisruptionMode indicates that all pods in the group must be disrupted together. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="single" type="any">
              WorkloadPodGroupSingleDisruptionMode indicates that individual pods can be disrupted independently. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
          </Expandable>
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="resource_claims" type="list(object)">
          ResourceClaims defines which ResourceClaims may be shared among Pods in the Job. Pods consume the devices allocated to a PodGroup's claim by defining a claim in its own Spec.ResourceClaims that matches the PodGroup's claim exactly. The claim must have the same name and refer to the same ResourceClaim or ResourceClaimTemplate. At most 4 claims may be set, matching the limit on the resulting PodGroup. This list is immutable after creation: entries may neither be added, removed, nor modified. Optional.
          <Expandable title="properties">
            <ResponseField name="name" type="string">
              The name of the ResourceClaim that this job's pod references in its scheduling configuration. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="resource_claim_name" type="string">
              The name of the ResourceClaim object that this entry references. The job's pods will use the resources from this claim when they are scheduled. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="resource_claim_template_name" type="string">
              The name of the ResourceClaimTemplate in the same namespace that will be used to generate a ResourceClaim for this claim. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
            </ResponseField>
          </Expandable>
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="scheduling_constraints" type="object">
          WorkloadPodGroupSchedulingConstraints defines leaf-level scheduling constraints, such as topology. Optional.
          <Expandable title="properties">
            <ResponseField name="topology" type="list(object)">
              topology specifies desired topological placements for all pods within the pod group. If unset, no topology placement is requested. Optional.
              <Expandable title="properties">
                <ResponseField name="key" type="string">
                  The topology key that identifies the domain to spread workloads across, such as 'kubernetes.io/hostname' or 'topology.kubernetes.io/zone'. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
                </ResponseField>
              </Expandable>
            </ResponseField>
          </Expandable>
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="scheduling_policy" type="object">
          WorkloadPodGroupSchedulingPolicy defines the scheduling policy for a group of pods managed by a workload controller. Exactly one policy must be set. Optional.
          <Expandable title="properties">
            <ResponseField name="basic" type="any">
              WorkloadPodGroupBasicSchedulingPolicy indicates standard Kubernetes scheduling behavior. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="gang" type="object">
              WorkloadPodGroupGangSchedulingPolicy defines the parameters for gang (all-or-nothing) scheduling. Optional.
              <Expandable title="properties">
                <ResponseField name="min_count" type="number">
                  minCount is the minimum number of pods that must be scheduled at the same time for the scheduler to admit the entire group. This field is optional. If it is not specified, the controller should inject a context-specific sane default (e.g., parallelism for a Job). If set, it must be a positive integer. Optional.
                </ResponseField>
              </Expandable>
            </ResponseField>
          </Expandable>
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="selector" type="object">
      A label selector is a label query over a set of resources. The result of matchLabels and matchExpressions are ANDed. An empty label selector matches all objects. A null label selector matches no objects. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="match_expressions" type="list(object)">
          matchExpressions is a list of label selector requirements. The requirements are ANDed. Optional.
          <Expandable title="properties">
            <ResponseField name="key" type="string">
              The label key that the match expression applies to. This is the key part of a Kubernetes label selector requirement. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="operator" type="string">
              The operator represents a key's relationship to a set of values. Valid operators are In, NotIn, Exists, and DoesNotExist. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="values" type="list(string)">
              A list of label values that the selector matches. Required for the 'In' and 'NotIn' operators; must be empty for 'Exists' and 'DoesNotExist'. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
            </ResponseField>
          </Expandable>
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="match_labels" type="map(string)">
          matchLabels is a map of &#123;key,value&#125; pairs. A single &#123;key,value&#125; in the matchLabels map is equivalent to an element of matchExpressions, whose key field is "key", the operator is "In", and the values array contains only "value". The requirements are ANDed. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="success_policy" type="object">
      SuccessPolicy describes when a Job can be declared as succeeded based on the success of some indexes. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="rules" type="list(object)" required>
          rules represents the list of alternative rules for the declaring the Jobs as successful before `.status.succeeded &gt;= .spec.completions`. Once any of the rules are met, the "SuccessCriteriaMet" condition is added, and the lingering pods are removed. The terminal state for such a Job has the "Complete" condition. Additionally, these rules are evaluated in order; Once the Job meets one of the rules, other rules are ignored. At most 20 elements are allowed. Required.
          <Expandable title="properties">
            <ResponseField name="succeeded_count" type="number">
              The minimum number of pods that must succeed for the job to be considered successful under this success policy rule. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="succeeded_indexes" type="string">
              A comma-separated list of index ranges (e.g., '0-2,5') that must have succeeded for this success policy rule to match. This field is used with indexed Jobs, and the rule is satisfied when all indexes in the specified set are in the Succeeded phase. The format supports individual indices and ranges (e.g., '0,2-4'). Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
            </ResponseField>
          </Expandable>
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="suspend" type="bool">
      suspend specifies whether the Job controller should create Pods or not. If a Job is created with suspend set to true, no Pods are created by the Job controller. If a Job is suspended after creation (i.e. the flag goes from false to true), the Job controller will delete all active Pods associated with this Job. Users must design their workload to gracefully handle this. Suspending a Job will reset the StartTime field of the Job, effectively resetting the ActiveDeadlineSeconds timer too. Defaults to false. Optional.
    </ResponseField>

      <Expandable title="properties">

          ObjectMeta is metadata that all persisted resources must have, which includes all objects users must create. Optional.

            <ResponseField name="annotations" type="map(string)">

They are not queryable and should be preserved when modifying objects. More info: https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/overview/working-with-objects/annotations Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="creation_timestamp" type="string">

This may be slow for large volumes, but allows mixing privileged and unprivileged Pods sharing the same volume on the same node. "MountOption" mounts all eligible Pod volumes with `-o context` mount option. This requires all Pods that share the same volume to use the same SELinux label. It is not possible to share the same volume among privileged and unprivileged Pods. Eligible volumes are in-tree FibreChannel and iSCSI volumes, and all CSI volumes whose CSI driver announces SELinux support by setting spec.seLinuxMount: true in their CSIDriver instance. Other volumes are always re-labelled recursively. If not specified, "MountOption" is used. This field affects only Pods that have SELinux label set, either in PodSecurityContext or in SecurityContext of all containers. All Pods that use the same volume should use the same seLinuxChangePolicy, otherwise some pods can get stuck in ContainerCreating state.                     </ResponseField>
                    <ResponseField name="storage_policy_name" type="string">
                      Name of the vSphere storage policy that governs the volume placement and characteristics. Optional; if not set, the default storage policy is used. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
                    </ResponseField>
                    <ResponseField name="volume_path" type="string">
                      Path that identifies the vSphere volume, typically the vmdk path such as '[datastore1] vmdisk1'. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
                    </ResponseField>
                  </Expandable>
                </ResponseField>
              </Expandable>
            </ResponseField>
          </Expandable>
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="ttl_seconds_after_finished" type="number">
      ttlSecondsAfterFinished limits the lifetime of a Job that has finished execution (either Complete or Failed). If this field is set, ttlSecondsAfterFinished after the Job finishes, it is eligible to be automatically deleted. When the Job is being deleted, its lifecycle guarantees (e.g. finalizers) will be honored. If this field is unset, the Job won't be automatically deleted. If this field is set to zero, the Job becomes eligible to be deleted immediately after it finishes. Optional.
    </ResponseField>
  </Expandable>
</ResponseField>

<ResponseField name="status" type="object">
  JobStatus represents the current state of a Job. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes.
  <Expandable title="properties">
    <ResponseField name="active" type="number">
      The number of pending and running pods which are not terminating (without a deletionTimestamp). The value is zero for finished jobs. Optional.
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="completed_indexes" type="string">
      completedIndexes holds the completed indexes when .spec.completionMode = "Indexed" in a text format. The indexes are represented as decimal integers separated by commas. The numbers are listed in increasing order. Three or more consecutive numbers are compressed and represented by the first and last element of the series, separated by a hyphen. For example, if the completed indexes are 1, 3, 4, 5 and 7, they are represented as "1,3-5,7". Optional.
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="completion_time" type="string">
      Time is a wrapper around time.Time which supports correct marshaling to YAML and JSON. Wrappers are provided for many of the factory methods that the time package offers. Optional.
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="conditions" type="list(object)">
      The latest available observations of an object's current state. When a Job fails, one of the conditions will have type "Failed" and status true. When a Job is suspended, one of the conditions will have type "Suspended" and status true; when the Job is resumed, the status of this condition will become false. When a Job is completed, one of the conditions will have type "Complete" and status true. A job is considered finished when it is in a terminal condition, either "Complete" or "Failed". A Job cannot have both the "Complete" and "Failed" conditions. Additionally, it cannot be in the "Complete" and "FailureTarget" conditions. The "Complete", "Failed" and "FailureTarget" conditions cannot be disabled. More info: https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/workloads/controllers/jobs-run-to-completion/ Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="last_probe_time" type="string">
          The last time the condition was probed or checked by the system. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="last_transition_time" type="string">
          The last time the condition transitioned from one status to another, as a string in RFC 3339 format. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="message" type="string">
          A human-readable message indicating details about the condition, such as why the job is in this state. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="reason" type="string">
          A machine-readable, short reason for the condition's last transition, typically a CamelCase string (e.g., 'JobComplete'). Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="status" type="string">
          The status of the condition, one of 'True', 'False', or 'Unknown'. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="type" type="string">
          The type of condition for the batch job, such as 'Complete' or 'Failed'. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="failed" type="number">
      The number of pods which reached phase Failed. The value increases monotonically. Optional.
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="failed_indexes" type="string">
      FailedIndexes holds the failed indexes when spec.backoffLimitPerIndex is set. The indexes are represented in the text format analogous as for the `completedIndexes` field, ie. they are kept as decimal integers separated by commas. The numbers are listed in increasing order. Three or more consecutive numbers are compressed and represented by the first and last element of the series, separated by a hyphen. For example, if the failed indexes are 1, 3, 4, 5 and 7, they are represented as "1,3-5,7". The set of failed indexes cannot overlap with the set of completed indexes. Optional.
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="ready" type="number">
      The number of active pods which have a Ready condition and are not terminating (without a deletionTimestamp). Optional.
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="start_time" type="string">
      Time is a wrapper around time.Time which supports correct marshaling to YAML and JSON. Wrappers are provided for many of the factory methods that the time package offers. Optional.
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="succeeded" type="number">
      The number of pods which reached phase Succeeded. The value increases monotonically for a given spec. However, it may decrease in reaction to scale down of elastic indexed jobs. Optional.
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="terminating" type="number">
      The number of pods which are terminating (in phase Pending or Running and have a deletionTimestamp). Optional.
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="uncounted_terminated_pods" type="object">
      UncountedTerminatedPods holds UIDs of Pods that have terminated but haven't been accounted in Job status counters. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="failed" type="list(string)">
          failed holds UIDs of failed Pods. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="succeeded" type="list(string)">
          succeeded holds UIDs of succeeded Pods. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
  </Expandable>
</ResponseField>
